March 27, 1988

Your Ref: FIN/2286/ORG

Dear Sir,

This is a reply to your letter dated March 1st, 1988, in
reference to alleged "UFO" sightings in the South Yorkshire
area during February of this year.

First of all I would like to thankyou very much for the
information you provided me with. However, in reference to your
suggestion that the object seen in the Sheffield and Rotherham
area on Feb. 2nd was “a turboprop commuter aircraft or light
transport..[which could] fly directly from East Midlands to
Leeds Bradford airports", I have made enquiries with the two
civil airports involved (see copies attached), as well as
Manchester Airport - all of whom deny convincingly that it was
one of their aircraft that was involved.

At the same time I made enquiries with the RAF bases at
Linton-on-Ouse (York), Church Fenton, Binbrook and Leeming -
all of whom deny that any of their aircraft have been
responsible for the sightings; a letter from the MOD dated 15
March to my colleague confirms that "the RAF are
not aware of any air-defence activity on the dates and times in
question in the South Yorkshire area".

Further, I am enclosing copies of two reports made by
members of the public who saw this "UFO" on the night of
February 2nd which I would like you to consider in detail. FER
Sectio( QJ watched this object ~- undoubtedly the same as that seen
by others at Ecclesfield - through binoculars and positively
Section 40 | it as an aircraft. I have spoken at some length with

and he is certain that the object he saw was a

military aircraft of some kind and not a civilian light
[enclosed] describes

aircraft.
Further, the account >y
what he thinks was "a transporter plane such as a Hercules" as

responsible for these sightings. The third account is by
who saw both a light aircraft and an unknown light ,_,,

source on the night of either 3/4 FebruaryS (stk went Ge ledalts of GA),
I have also read several newspaper articles referring to

members of the “Yorkshire UFO Society” who claim that strange

lights were being pursued by military jets over

Rotherham/Wentworth area during January of this year. What is

your comment as regards these unlikely allegations?
These accounts are just three out of nearly one hundred that

I have now received from members of the public describing

objects they have observed in the sky over South Yorkshire

0
since the end of 1987. I do of course accept that the majority
of these sightings have conventional explanations such as
aircraft lights, bright stars & planets, etc; however, some of
the evidence does appear to suggest that some kind of
structured object - probably a military aircraft of some kind -
has been manoeuvring at low-level in this area. Unfortunately,
as no one seems to want to admit that this aircraft belongs to
them these sightings will eventually be added to the rapidly
growing UFO mythology!

In reference to your letter again, can I take it from your
comments about the Ecclesfield police sighting that the object
involved was picked up on radar at RAF Finningley? This is what
appears to be implied whatever way you wish to read it.

Finally, in reference to your suggestion that I “observe a
large airport at night in order to familiarise [myself] with
the many different size and patterns of light displayed by
aircraft" would it be possible for me to visit Finningley to
this end?

As I do speak to a large number of people who report "UFO
sightings" each year it would be very educational for me if I
could be shown light configurations commonly used on aircraft
taking part in night-exercises. I am willing to co-operate with
any conditions you may stipulate in relation to the above.

I would like to add that I have no preconceived notions
about "UFO sightings" and think that the majority are
explainable by ‘the appliance of science’.

I enclose an SAE and look forward to hearing from you in the
near future.

Yours Sincerely,

Thank you for your letter dated 3 March 1988. I am afraid that

we are unable to identify any aircraft that might explain your.

sighting on 2 February 1988, or on the 10th February. The only
aircraft controlled by Leeds Bradford Airport at the time you indic-
ated was inbound to LBA from the west having departed from Dublin.
Your suggested track from Rotherham to Ecclesfield is not a route
jikely to be used by aircraft operating to or from this airport.
Doncaster or Sandtoft airfields have, in the past, carried out
some night flying and may possibly have had traffic in the area
you mention.

The only aircraft that would route to Leeds Bradford from the
Sheffield area at the times you specify would fly either via the
airways system from the South and pass to the West of Sheffield,
near Penistone but usually above 4000 feet, or alternatively route
from the Finningley area passing well East of Sheffield.

Very low-level flights are more likely to be of military origin
Bor ERIS” would HOE Occur Over BuTIt Up “areas.” However, it is
net unknown for helicopters and other aircraft to operate at night
at fairly low level. In good visibility aircraft a long distance
away can seem close and of course you would not hear the noise.

However, we have no information about any military activities.

I am sorry I cannot be of any further assistance.

Airport Director

Registered in England No. 2065958
Registered Office: City Hall, Bradford, West Yorkshire, BD1 1HY

21 March 1985

Thank you for your letter dated 29 February 1988. We have investigated our
traffic patterns but are unable to confirm any movements.

You will no doubt be aware that Manchester is responsible for controlling
aircraft within Controlled Airspace and the nearest route to the area you
indicate would be some 10/15 miles to the North and flying at altitudes in
excess of 6,000 feet, mainly in an East/West direction.

I would remind you that aircraft both civil and military have flashing
navigation lights both red, white and sometimes strobe lights which give a
very strong flash/beam effect. These in conjunction with certain
cloud/weather conditions can provide unusual effects.

Yours sincerely

ATC Operations

15th March 1988

Dear Sir,

In reply to your letter of the 3rd March 1988 it would seem that the UFO seen on
the night of Tuesday, 2nd February 1988 would appear to have been travelling in a
westerly direction, in which case it is more than unlikely that it was an aircraft
en route between East Midlands Airport and Leeds Airport or vice versa. It would
seem more likely, if it was an aircraft, that it might be heading for Manchester
Airport or the aerodrome at Woodford, or might be low level military traffic in
which case the departure point and destination would be unknown to us.

I can throw no light on the activities observed on Wednesday, 10th February 1988
in the area between Hoyland and Penistone, except to add that both Hoyland and
Penistone lie beneath airway Bravo 1.

I can only add, and I am sure that you will be aware, that lights seen at night
can be very deceptive in terms of distance or estimated distance, particularly on
nights when there “ae visibility.

Yours faithfully,

Senior Air Traffic Control Officer

Registered in England and Waies under No. 2078271
Registered Otfice: East Midlands Airport, Castie Donington, Derby DE7 2SA, England

22 March 1988

L ?

Thank you for the opportunity to see the three investigation reports which you
passed to the Ministry of Defence for comment earlier this year. We were interested
to read of your findings and were impressed by the thoroughness of the reports.
However, having said that, I feel that I must say that the MOD has only a passing
interest in the UFO phenomenon, namely to ensure that UFOs do not pose a threat

to the security and defence of the nation and as such I am sorry to say that these
investigation reports are really wasted on us. If you wish to send further reports
to the MOD, then please do but I regret that due to other more defence orientated
work commitments and because we have no staff employed on the UFO subject full-
time we are not able to spend much, if any, time in analysing the detail given

in your reports, unless a clear threat to our security had been assessed.
Nevertheless, we were interested to see your reports and wish you luck with your
further endeavours.

Moving onto more specific points raised in your letter of 22 January, you mention
that you are concerned that alien or unidentified craft seem to be able to pass
undetected through the UR Air Defence Region. I must point out that the UK Air
Defence Region is extremely well protected and it is highly unlikely that an intruder
of conventional origin would be able to travel to the UK without being spotted.

I would suggest instead that many “alien" craft could in fact be either UK military
aircraft, civil aircraft, meteorological balloons and a host of other uncontentious
objects.

You also offered assistance with the investigation of unidentified objects seen

in the North of England. Although I thank you for your thoughtful offer, I regret
that despite seemingly to have merit in theory, we see little need for it from

our point of view.

You asked for information that we might have on sightings seen in Cheshire,
Staffordshire and Derbyshire during 1973-1979. Although it is possible that we
may have some information on sightings which relate to these details, I am sorry
to say that all of this information is now stored in MOD Central archives and
that it would take a disproportionate amount of time to locate them for you.

D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

/® March 1988

Thank you for your report of a possible Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sighting
that you witnessed on 17 January 1988.

I think it would be helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate
some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence
is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally

the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

However, any reports that we receive of sightings, which usually are limited
to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those who witnessed
them, are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible for
the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part of
their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do

not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any indepth investigations. TI am sure that you will appreciate

that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of the UK

against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so,

we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations,

unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified,

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.

i hope that this proves helpful.

Yours sincerely

R.R.PLR.G. is a small group of local
enthusiasts interested in the subject
of Psychic and other unexplained
Phenomenon. its aims are to collect,
research, discuss and in some cases
investigate incidents reported in the
Redhill, Reigate and surrounding area...

The group holds no fixed views and. tries
to bring an open-minded and scientific
approach to all its research and investi-
gations.

4ll information gathered by the group
is treated in strict confidence, and

no individual details will be published
unless permission has been granted.
(Pseudonyms will be used if desired).

The R.RLPLR.G. is affiliated to the
Association for the Selentific Study
of Anemolous Phenomena { ASSAP).

Thank you for your letter dated 7 March 1988. J note that the sighting to which
you referred in your earlier letter was observed on Saturday 3 January 1988
not 29 January 1988 as originally stated.

I have consulted with the Royal Air Force and I am able to confirm that they
did not carry out any exercises or search and rescue missions on the night in
question. Similarly, I regret that we did not receive any report (other than
your own) which corresponds to the details that you gave.

I hope that this proves helpful.

With reference to your letter of 17th February
about unidentified aerial activity I regret we are
unable to provide any information on the events you
describe. Our area of coverage does not extend
that far south. I suspect from the description it
could have been low flying aircraft but we have no
way of confirming this.

The MOD department you are already in contact
with is the correct authority to receive such reports.

Yours sincerely

February 17, 1988

Dear Sir,

I am a student at Sheffield University and have an interest
in observations of unusual aerial phenomena; for around ten
years I have been privately investigating instances of "UFO"
sightings in the South Yorkshire/Derbyshire area out of
scientific interest, and have recently begun to send my case
reports to the Secretariat (Air Staff) 2a at the MOD in London.

Iam writing in reference to a number of sightings of
unidentified aerial phenomena in the Sheffield/Rotherham area
on the night of Tuesday, February 2nd, 1988. I have collected a
number of independant sighting reports from this particular
night which appear to relate to the passage of a "V-shaped"
craft with a series of red lights over the northern suberbs of
Sheffield; it appeared to come from the Blackburn
Meadows/Rotherham area and passed over Ecclesfield where it was
scen and heard by police officers shortly before 8 p.m.

I have received reports from over 40 independent witnesses
in various parts of the northern suberbs of Sheffield
corroborating the details given above. I have also received
reports from a further 10 witnesses who saw a similar object -
this time triangular/diamond shaped, surrounded by red and
white lights and flying at very low level, in the area west of
Barnsley, and around Penistone, on the night of Wednesday,
February 10th, 1988.

My. reason for writing is to ask if you are able to identify
the object/s involved in the sightings above as a RAF/NATO
aircraft, or some kind of refuelling exercise of the kind that
recently caused similar sightings in the Hull area? Are you
also aware of any low-flying exercises by civilian
aircraft/helicopters, etc over the areas above on the nights in
question?

I would also be interested to hear whether you have received
any similar reports from members of the public in the South
Yorkshire/Derbyshire area recently, and if so if it would be
possible for you to pass on to me their names and addresses so
that further enquiries could be made with them.

I must add that I am interested in this subject out of
scientific curiosity, and feel that reports of such incidents
should be recorded and collated in report form in a way that
they could be of use to possible scientific investigation in
Future years.

I'm enclosing a Stamped Addressed Envelope and look forward
to hearing from you in the near future,

vf . io ff
Yours Sincere tes

D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

/s° March 1988

‘eo.
Thank you for your report which I received from RAF Finningley concerning a possible
Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sighting that you witnessed on the evening of
7 January 1988. JI have since established that there was no Royal Air Force air
activity in the area at the time and date that you specified and I regret that
because we do not carry out full-time studies or in-depth investigations into
the UFO phenomena, I am unable to offer you any explanation for what was seen.
However, I am able to confirm that we did receive a similar sighting report from
the Rotherham area, a copy of which is attached for your information. I note
that the witness of this particular report is also a member of YUFOs and you
might, therefore, find it useful if you compare notes.

I think you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate
some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence

is to determine whether or not UFOs present’ a threat to the security and defence

of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally

the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

The sighting reports we receive, such as yours, are referred to our staff in

the departments which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom

and they examine the reports as part of their normal duties.

Although we are able, in certain circumstances, to let you know if we have received
any information concerning particular unusual sightings, I regret that the release
of any material on the subject “en bloc" is not possible as, like other government
departments, all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act. As you may

be aware, this Act states that official files generally remain closed from public
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris

re~entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host
of other things.

I hope that this is helpful.

#3 March 1988

Thank you for your letter of 24 February 1988 which asked for any information
that the Ministry of Defence might have on 3 sightings seen in the South Yorkshire
area on 2, 10 and 18 February 1988.

As you already know, information that we have in the MOD on possible Unidentified
Flying Object (UFO) sightings is usually limited to copies of sighting reports
passed on to us by those who witnessed the incident. In this particular case,

I can confirm that we received 4 reports corresponding to the details you gave

in your letter. TI attach copies of these reports for your information, although
you will note, in line with normal practice, I have deleted any reference to

who may have originated the reports.

I would also like to confirm that the RAF are not aware of any air defence activity
on the dates and times in question in the South Yorkshire area. However, 1 regret
that I do not have the resources to undertake a full investigation into all possible
air activity which might have occurred in the region.

Lastly, I think it would be helpful if I point out that the Police do not come

under the umbrella of the Ministry of Defence, but under the control of the Home
Office.

I hope that this is of help.

Uns Gevneky

following our instructions. He is likewise loving My
Mother with this baby. Note, how tender he is toward
her. He did not realize that the paternal love for one's
child was so strong. He is aware of the feeling of great
protectivity for this baby, and would do anything for
her. The baby too feels the love of her parents, and
she too loves her mother and father.

Long have My children believed that as soon as a
seeker begins to change organizations and enters a
new teaching, from that moment on, the student is 2
within the same frequency as the other students, It is
believed that it is only necessary to join and accept the
new teachings, now the student Is within the 7th
degree, It is like being promoted to a higher grade, «7
The moment you are promoted into a new Class, you
are now in the grade of the entire class, This is the
way it is done within the school system. However, it
has not been understood that when aspirants enter into
the 7th degree of these teachings, the students who
have been studying within these teachings have
already had the 5th and 6th degree teachings dissolved
before the new teachings can be registered within the
spirit body. Many members have discovered that,
although they would study the knowledge taught by
the ascended beings, they were unable to practice
what they had studied. Neither could they incorporate
what the other students already had registered within
te their own spirits.

When first becoming a new student, it fs like having
a house already filled with old, dilapidated furniture that
is ready to be discarded. But before new furniture is
moved into the house, the outdated furniture must be
removed. The new, modern, and beautiful furniture is
to replace the old furniture within the same house. The
old furniture would be the symbcis of the former
teachings, with the attendant beliefs and habit patterns pi
learned within the 5th and éth degrees. In the
beginning, it is necessary for the disciple to study
awhile to determine for himself whether the new
furniture is desired. Many are comfortable to remain in
their former degrees, where they have settled into a
comfortable, familiar pattern of their spirits, These may
not desire to make the necessary changes. Often,
there are studenis who will clutter up their rooms and
just move the new furniture to remain with the old.

STATES OF CONSCIOUSNESS

Consciousness is the state of each one’s awareness
of himself and his environment. All Is based on
accumulated knowledge, experience, training and the
sum total-of all the states of consciousness an individual
has passed through. Parents have the same state of
consciousness which they have passed through. The
children are brought into their area where their children,
and the surrounding people within their environment are
likewise carrying the sum total of each one's
consciousness. Within each area, My children have
served Me throughout many lifetimes, Since My Father
knew each one's state of consciousness perfectly, He
accepted the service in each one.

In the past, the children simply followed and imitated
their parents, learning from them to the extent of their
cumulative knowledge. The parents directed and
controlled their own children to follow the image of the
parents for generations.

My dearly beloved ones, within these teachings, a
great change has occurred. Where in the past, the
parents observed their children in all that happened;
now the children would observe their parents and
review their former states of consciousness through the
study of their parents. My children would now observe
the parents from the time they were able to recall the
scenes of the past. However, other children in the
family remained with the same state of consciousness as
their parents. But at varied times, all the children
wearied of the continued, unchanged ways and same
patterns of behavior; and would,at a later date, make
the necessary change in moving to another country.

Thank you for your letter of 3 February 1988 which asked for any information
that the Ministry of Defence might possess on a possible Unidentified Flying
Object (UFO) sighting seen in the Kensington area on 16 January 1988.

I believe the incident you are referring to is the same as that reported in

the Express and the Independent Newspapers on 6 January 1988, which suggested
that the incident occurred on Sunday 3 January 1988. Apart from this, I am
afraid we have no other record of this incident. TI am sorry that you feel that
the British Government and others are attempting to suppress information about
UFOs from the general public and can assure you that this is certainly not the
case in Britain. As I said in my last letter, the UK Ministry of Defence's

main concern is to establish whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security
and defence of the United Kingdom. if we feel they do nat, which is normally

the case, then we have neither the time nor the resources to investigate, identify
or carry out any in-depth studies of the objects or into the UFO phenomenon

as a whole.

We were grateful for and interested to read your extracts from various books

on the UFO subject but as both incidents occurred in the USA we are unable to
offer any useful comment. The statement attributed to a former British Diplomat
and Intelligence Officer is surprising and at least as far as this Government

is concerned, total nonsense,

Finally, having also received your letter of 3 January 1988, which was addressed
to Secretariat Division 8 I should explain that this is the old (pre 1984)

title of what is now Secretariat(Air Staff) (Sec(AS)), who remain the only MOD
point of contact for enquiries about UFOs.

I hope you will find this helpful.

Thank you for your letter dated 5 January 1988 which has been passed to me for
attention by the Director of Public Relations (RAF), Air Commodore Hunter.

Before I answer the specific points raised in your 1985 letters concerning the
sighting seen at Lowtherville, I would like to echo the feelings of Air Commodore
Hunter as stated in his letter of 7 January 1988. The Ministry of Defence is
certainly a very large government department and unfortunately it occasionally
does happen that letters may not be directed to the branch which has the necessary
knowledge to answer them. Having said that, however, I am sorry that the letter
to the Head of the Naval Staff Secretariat was not answered to your satisfaction.

Tam also concerned that you feel that the Ministry of Defence deliberately
tries to be obstructive as this is certainly not the case. All letters that

we received on this particular subject are answered, although this occasionally
may take time due to other work commitments.

Although all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act, which states that
official files should generally remain closed from public viewing for 30 years
after the last action has been taken, we are able to answer any specific queries
investigators may have about particular sightings which have occurred recently.
We can then let you know whether or not we have a report on the incident and

if so, what it contains. However, I think it is important that I explain that
whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about Unidentified Flying
Object (UFO) sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is ta determine
whether or not they present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not normally the case, no
attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed solely for

the purpose of studying reports of these objects and we have no staff who are
employed on the subject full time, This being so, we do not have the necessary
resources to undertake any further investigations or general surveys. The reports
that we receive, which are usually limited to the brief details of sightings
passed onto us by those who witnessed them or by investigators such as yourself,
are referred to MOD staff in the departments which are responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part of their

normal duties.
+ Moving onto the Lowtherville sighting itself, I have now seen copies of your
original letters and have been able to look into the incident to see if there
areé any plaasine reasons for the sighting. Unfortunately, however, I have
established from the Naval Staffs that they have no record of Naval exercises
for that area for 19 May 1985 that would have included the use of low flying
objects over the Isle of Wight. Similarly, I have not found any evidence to
suggest that RAF activity could have caused this incident.

I hope you will find this useful.

Thank you for your letter of 15 February 1988 which asked if the Royal Air Force
had been involved in any military exercise in the Cheshire and Shropshire areas,
which might bave caused an unusual sighting between Nantwich and Whitchurch on

23 January 1988. Although I note that you call this a Saturday; it was actually

a Friday; I have taken the nights cf both 29 and 30 January 1988 into consideration.

Po have established that there was no military air activity in this region on

those particular nights. From a military point of view, the only reasonable

explanation for a landing oecuring so close to midnight would be a search and
.

rescue operation but I can confirm that none occurred on either 29 or 30 January 1988
in this particular area.

Moving on to the pieces of debris that you recovered from the “landing sight",
we would be curious to know the results of your analysis if anything interesting

was revealed,

IT hope that this proves useful. oy .

thank you for your letter of 7 February 1988 to National Air Traffic Services (NATS)
which has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for attention.

You asked if there was any air activity in the Moreton-in-Marsh area which might
have caused the unusual sighting on 18 December 1987. As was mentioned to you
in the reply from NATS, we regret that there was no known aerial activity in
that particular area and as such, we are unable to offer you any explanation

for the sighting that was seen.

You may tind it helpful if I explain that whilst we do réceive and co-ordinate

some information about Unidentified Flying Object (UFO) sightings, the main

concern of the Ministry of Befence is to determine whether or not UFOs present

a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge

that they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to
investigate or identify the objects. The sighting reports we recieve, which

usually are limited to the brief details of the incident passed to us by those

who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible
for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part

of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do

not carry out full-time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any in depth investigations. I am sure that you will appreciate

that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of the UK

against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so,

we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations unless

a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that Many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball Lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorclogical balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.

To hope that this is helpful.

Ye Sree

Tup U.S. Senator
Saw Two UFOs

One of the most powerful U.S. senators in modern
history actually eyewitnessed two UFOs while on a
fact-finding trip through Russia in 1955 — and the U.S.
governinent has kept the
sightings a secret for more

than three decades!

The incredible encounter is de-
tailed in 12 top secret CIA, FBI
and Air Force r. ports —- only re-
cently declassified — which have
been obtained by The EN-(;

QUIRER.

Those startling reports re-
veal that Sen. Richard B. Rus-
sell Jr. (D.-Ga.) — then chair-
man of the Armed Services
Committee — was on a Soviet
train when he spotted a disc-
shaped aircraft taking off
near the tracks. He hurriedly
called his military aide and
interpreter to the window —
and they saw the UFO plus
- another one that appeared a
minute later.

The astonished trio re-
ported the sightings to the
U.S. Air Force as soon as
they were out of Russia.

“The three observers were
firmly convinced that they
saw a genuine flying saucer
or flying disc,” says an Air
Force Intelligence report
dated Oct. 14, 1955, and tlassi-
fied top secret at the time.

Sen. Russell served 38
years in the Senate. He was
its senior member and one of
the most influential senators
at the time of his death in
1971, He was chairman of the
Armed Services Committee
from 1951 to 1969, and unsuec-
cessfully sought the Demo-
cratic Presidential nomina-
tion in 1952.

The mind-boggling docu-
ments detailing his UFO en-
counter were made available
exclusively to The ENQUIR-

ER by the Fund for UFO Re. *

search and its chairman, US.
Navy physicist Dr. Bruce
Maccabee. Several key docu-
ments were obtained by the
group through the Freedom
of Information Act.

Thank you for your letter of 6 February 1988 to the Ministry of Defence which
has been passed to me for attention in my capacity as the MOD point of contact
on the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).

I read with interest your unusual experience, although I believe you may find

it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information
about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine
whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the

United Kingdom, Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the

case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed sclely for

the purpose of studying reports of such objects and we have no staff who are

employed on the subject full time. The sighting reports we receive which normally

are limited only to the brief details of the incident passed on to us by those

who witnessed them, are referred to our staff in the departments which are responsible
for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part

of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications, we do

not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any in-depth investigations. f= am sure that you will appreciate

that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of

the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This

being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations
unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified,

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive, that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball Lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things.

I hope that this proves helpful.

Your Reference

Our Reference
D/CS(R)1/6/1/3
Date

\Q February 1988

Thank you for your letter of 1 February 1988 on the subject of material on Un-
Identified Flying Objects held by the Public Record Office.

Our enquiries have indicated that there are no Ministry of Defence records on
this subject in the PRO. A House of Lords debate on the topic on 4 March 1982
revealed that all UFO records prior to 1967 had been destroyed. Those created
after that date are subject to the provisions of the Public Records Acts and
would not be available for public scrutiny until 1998 at the earliest.

My colleagues at the Cabinet Office have also searched through their records
but again there are no Cabinet Office files in the PRO on this particular subject.

It seems, therefore, that there are no official papers on this subject presently
available in the Public Record Office and in their absence I can only refer

you to commercially available books and publications which may indicate other
primary source material.

I am sorry not to be of more assistance but may I wish you success in your research.

Yours sincerely

Thank you for your recent letter to the Ministry of Defence which gave a description
of an object that you saw from your house during February 1986.

We are grateful to you for writing about your experience, although I think it
would be helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some
information about sightings of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs), the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not U¥Os present
a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge
that they do and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to
investigate or identify the objects. JI am sure that you will appreciate that
defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of the

UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries and this being
so, we could not justify the use of defence Funds on scientific investigations,
unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange

things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
ve-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorites, meterorolgical- balloons, aircraft lgihts, aircraft at unusual angles

or a host of other rare conjunctions.

I.am net sure, however, that this will be of much comfort to you in explaining

your own experience and I am sorry that we cannot be more helpful. I quite
appreciate that some sightings where rational explanations are not readily available
can be very disturbing and I hope that this goes some way in reassuring you
that, the majority of unusual object sightings have some plausible reason,

I hope that this is helpful.

You Sacaeseti,

/§ February 19838

Thank you for your letter of 26 January 1988 which I read with interest.

While I am quite happy to provide you with a Ministry of Defence statement of
policy on the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) for 1988, I do not
think you will thank me for repeating large sections of my letter to you dated
12 August 1987 which set out our policy for 1987. As you will gather, little
has changed to alter our approach to the subject of UFOs and as such our policy
on the subject remains essentially unchanged.

Nevertheless, to recap, our main concern is to determine whether or not UFOs
present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless

we judge that they do and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made
by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on

the subject full time. This being so, we do not have the resources to undertake
any further investigations or general surveys. The sighting reports we receive,
which usually are limited only to the brief details of the incident passed

onto ue by those whoJare re are paaek nN to our staffs in the departments which are
responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports
as:part of their-normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited te possible defence implications, we do

not, carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do we have the resources
to undertake any in-depth investigations. I am sure that you will appreciate

that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of the UK

against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so

we could not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations,

uniess a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.
As far as the release of this material en bloc is concerned all MOD files are
subject to the Public Records Act. Under this Act official files generally
remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been

taken. However, we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let you know whether

we have a report on the incident and if se, what it contains.

I hope that this is of help.
T: The Ministry Of Defence.,

Whitehall, |

February 8, 1988

Your reference: D/Sec (AS) 12/3.

Dear Sir,

Thankyou for your note dated February 3, 1988.

In reference to the Barnsley photographs of August 1987 -
the two photographs I enclosed with the report are
unfortunately rather poor quality reproductions of the
originals, and I thought that I would write to let you know
that if you find the incident of sufficient interest, higher
quality prints of all four of the sequence of photographs can
be provided. is reluctant to part with his original
negatives for any length of time unless a written guarantee of
return is provided, so if you wish to scrutinise these I would
advise you to contact him directly at the address provided in
the report. .

You are welcome to retain the material I sent indefinately
for your own files, as I have my own copies.

Finally, a very interesting sighting by police-officers at
Ecclesfield Police~Station in Sheffield has just come to my
attention, having been reported in the local press in Sheffield
on Saturday, February 6th, 1988. The report was made by police-
constables and Section 40 who have reported seeing
a V-shaped object carrying flashing red lights hovering over
their patrol~car near the police-station at Ecclesfield.

I have been in contact with the police-station in question
in order to obtain details of the above incident, but they have
apparently been deluged with enquiries from the press, TV news
and "UFO societies", and have decided not to release details to
anyone at this moment in time.

My reason for mentioning the above sighting is to ask
whether you have received any notification directly from the
police at Ecclesfield, Sheffield, regarding this sighting. I
would like to speak to the officers in question to obtain
comprehensive details of the incidents, in order to compile a
report on the matter that would be of use to scientific bodies
and yourself. I have informed the Duty-Sergeant that I have no
connections with the press and will respect the anonymity of
those involved.

I would like to hear your views on the above, and look
forward to hearing from you soon,

Yours Sincerely,

Main Building Whitehall SW1A 2HB

Your Reference
Our Reference
D/Sec(AS )12/3
Date

A, January 1988

Thank you for your most recent letter, which asked for information that the
Ministry of Defence might have on possible Unidentified Flying Object (UFO)
sightings seen in Telford on 23 October 1983 and what I expect is meant to be
3 January 1988. Unfortunately, you did not mention the year.

As stated in my letter dated 14 December 1987, the Air Staff Secretariat does
not have the necessary available resources to undertake searches through our
archive material to answer general queries which have little detail, covar wide
timespans and which ask about sightings which have not occurred recently. f
regret, therefore, that because of the time that it would have taken, we have
not been able to look through our files to establish whether or not any sightings
were seen in Birmingham or Telford in 1986 and 1987, However, I have looked
through our current records and would like to confirm that we did not receive

a report for the Telford or Birmingham area for 3 January 1988.

Moving on to your other points, again I would like to remind you that the Air
Staff Secretariat does not have the facilities and information available to
provide answers to a wide variety of questions not directly connected to the
Royal Air Force. Nevertheless, I suggest, should you wish to reply to the letter
you received from Washington DC, that you write back to the address on their
letterhead.

I am sorry that I am unable to help further.

Ypnnee SAAC,

From: Section 40, | Secretariat (Air Staff)2a, ,
ge i “he CoH
MINISTRY OF DEFENCE room Section 40 | de ial

Main Building Whitehail London SW1A 2HB
Telephone: (Direct Dialling)
(Switchboard)

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

% January 1988

Thank you for your letter dated 5 January which has been passed to
me for attention.

I was interested to read about the incident witnessed by BEQIOMEE
and her husband in Pool-in-Wharfedale,and if BRRIEMEEWishes to pass
on more details of the sighting I would be most happy to receive

them. However, I think that it would-be helpful if I explain that
whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO
sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine
whether or not Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) present a threat

to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge
that they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made

by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.

The Ministry of Defence has no department which is appointed solely
for the purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who
are employed on the subject full time. The sighting reports we
receive, which usually are limited only to the brief details of the
incident passed on to us by those who witnessed them, are referred
to our staff in the departments which are responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part
of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
we do not carry out full time studies into the UFO phenomenon nor do
we have the resources to undertake any in depth investigations. I am
sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are already fully
committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could
not justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations,
unless a clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.
Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe
that rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the earth's
atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological
balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of
other things.

I hope that this is of help.

“yf oe
Fann Goatent e F

UFO ENQUIRY FROM

Reference: CSA 12/3 677/87 dated 22 Dee 87

l- Your minute at Reference sought advice on a letter
CSA had recently received co: Ea
Stockton om Tees which enquired about our involvement
with the UFO phenomenon.

2. 1 attach a suitable draft reply which 1 suggest
should be signed by you as PS.

Sec{AS)2

DRAFT

Thank you for your letter of 11 December to Sir Richard Norman, who has passed it

to me for reply.

You asked particularly about the subject of UFOs and indicated that you would
like to work in this area. I am not clear whether you have corresponded before with
the MOB branch that deals with UFO enquiries, which is in fact part of the Air Staff,
not the Scientific Staff. There is however no unit that deals with UFOs and indeed no
full time staff covering UFOs. Instead several areas of the Ministry such as the Air
Staff, fhe Scientific staff and the Intelligence staff hold records on UFO matters
for their own purposes. The only real MOD interest however relates to whether or not
UFOs might present a threat to the security of this country, which is not normally
thought to be the case. This being so we could not justify the use of additional
defence funds and resources for any major investigation into the source of any
particular phenomena, and our records are largely confined to details of sightings, as

reported to us.

i am sorry if this comes as a disappointment to you but would add that if you are
interested more generally in a career with the scientific civil service you may wish
to get in touch with the Civil Service Commission at Alencon Link who will be able to
give you more information as to the sorts of jobs which are available.

I hope you will find this helpful.

PS/CSA

DEPARTMENT OF CIVIL ENGINEERING, ASTON UNIVERSITY

COMBINED HONOURS: WATER RESOURCES OPTION

OUTLINE SYLLABUS

YEAR 1.

HISTORY OF WATER DEVELOPMENT
Historical review of early water developments; development of

water supply and sewerage systems; water legislation and
administraton; the role of water in national development.

HYDROLOGY
The hydrological cycle; causes, variation and measurement of

precipitation, evapotranspiration, infiltration, snowmelt and
sediment transport; groundwater movement and aquifers.

SOIL SCIENCE
Formation, properties and composition of soil; distribution,

classification and mapping of soils.

RIVER BASIN GEOMORPHOLOGY
River basin form and processes; morphometry; estuaries and

deltas.

LIMNOLOGY

Chemical and biological aspects of water; ecology of natural
waters; anthropogenic influences on aquatic ecosystems;
assessment and surveiliance of water quality; biology of water
supply and used-water treatment; water borne and water related

diseases.

COMPUTING
Introduction to computers; introduction to BASIC; program

learning course in BASIC.

COURSEWORK
i) Water Resources Development Project: - preparation for and

participation in a simulated Public Inquiry.
ii) Hydrology and Limnology Laboratory Classes.
YEAR 2.

STATISTICS
Probability and basic statistics; cross and serial correlation;

linear regression; analysis of time series.

SURVEYING
Chain survey; use of level and theodolite; hydrographic

surveying.

FLUID MECHANICS

Hydrostatics; friction flow in pipes and analysis of pipe
networks; measurement of flow in pipes and open channels;
Manning and Chezy equations and their applications; gradually
varied flow; hydraulic jump and its use as an energy dissipator.

ECONOMICS

Price theory and resource allocation; present worth and
discounted cash flow; economic objectives and project appraisal;
evaluation of costs and benefits; economic and financial
feasibility; project selection.

WATER AND SANITATION IN DEVELOPING COUNTRIES

Social and political influences; aid; rural and urban water
supply and treatment; well construction and hand~pumps; excreta
disposal systems; waste water treatment, refuse collection and
disposal; engineering control of arthropod vectors; dams;
irrigation and health.

PUBLIC HEALTH ENGINEERING

Water supply: demand, treatment and distribution.

Wastewater: processing and disposal of industrial and domestic
wastes: sewage treatment; sewerage systems.

Recovery of natural waters.

COURSEWORK

i) Surveying projects.

ii) Fluid Mechanics Laboratory Exercises.

iii) Flood Economics Project.

iv) One week FIELD COURSE in Wales during long vacation.
YEAR _3

HYDROLOGICAL MODELLING AND SYSTEM SIMULATION

Data validation and analysis; conceptual rainfall/runoff models;
stchastic streamflow and rainfall models; multi-site models;
model testing; optimisation of simple systems by linear and
dynamic programming; simulationof complex regional water
systems; optimisation of development and operating policies by
system simulation.

RIVER MANAGEMENT, IRRIGATION AND LAND DRAINAGE

Flood protection works; flood prediction and warning; river
training works; navigation; estimation of water requirements for
crops; irrigation techniques; water distribution systems for
irrigation; land drainage; reclamation of land subject to water
logging and salinization.

REMOTE SENSING

Optical and electronic remote sensing systems; remote sensing
methods in hydrology, pollution control and land use studies;
image analysis; evaluation and interpretation of systems.

GROUNDWATER DEVELOPMENT

Origins and location of underground waters; estimation of
natural recharge; flow of groundwater; mathematical and analogue
models; safe yield from aquifers; groundwater exploitation.

ADVANCED HYDROLOGY
Flood and drought studies; climate and change; special factors

in arid and tropical hydrology.

COURSEWORK

i) Groundwater Exercise

ii} Hydrological modelling exercise

iii) Optimisation exercise

iv) FINAL YEAR PROJECT {selection from a range of titles)

HH January 1988

UFO ENQUIRY -

1. Please find attached some papers detailing the alleged sighting of a UFO
over the Isle of Wight on the evening of Sunday 19 May 1985.

2. I understand that you may still have some information about any exercises
taking place in the area at the time, and I would be grateful for any details
that you could provide.

3. It has been suggested that the activity referred to by the local police may
have been a radar tracking exercise, as I understand that these take place on

a regular basis in the area coneerned. If this was the case, then it seems possible
that the object seen was a target drogue being towed by a FRADU aircraft. I assume
that the drogue would be lit at night.

4, As the local MP is now involved, I would be grateful for your early comments
on the papers and my questions above, together with any further thoughts you may
have on the subject. Could I ask for a response by COP 29 January?

0a

Thank you for your letter dated 5 January 1988 in which you

voice a number of complaints about MOD handling of your enquiries
about the Lowtherville IOW UFO report. I have taken the liberty
of passing your letter on to my colleague RRR who deals
with such matters. You will appreciate that this is a very

large Ministry and that it is by no means a certainty that

a matter communicated to one department will be known to another.
Had your original message been addressed to the correct quarter,
I am confident that it would have received prompt attention.

I cannot agree that a letter to Commander-in-Chief Naval Home
Command constitutes a Ministry of Defence Record. Nor, I imagine
would you, on careful reflection.

You should note that for further enquiries, you should address
all correspondence to:

Secretariat (Air Staff) 2a
Room

Ministry of Defence

Main Building

Whitehall

London

SW1A 2HB

I know maesection 40 Fa. be delighted to deal with any enquiries

such as yours which are correctly addressed to him.

Ministry of Defence

Whitehall

November 27th 1985

Dear Sir,

Iam writing to you on behalf of the British UFO Research Association,
a society that collects, investigates and usually finds ordinary explanations
for reports of UFOs. I am investigating a very interesting report by a
resident of the Isle of Wight. At 20.40 Hrs on 19th May 1985, he was walking
on the Downs above Lowtherville, Ventnor (OSGR 554777) when he first
observed an object he thought was a parachute, approximately one kilometer
to the south-east (probably above the sea). The object rose up and began
travelling towards the startled witness. The object hovered for a few
seconds over nearby houses, and resolved itself as a disk shaped object,
with a flange, two hazy lights on its upper surface, and a slight glow. As
' the object passed within about I00 feet of the witness, he noted that it
was only a few feet across and made a slight swooshing noise as it passed
him. The object then disappeared over a hedge to the north west of the

witness, having been in his view for nearly five minutes. !
During the course of my investigation, I have written to the local
police, the Isle of Wight airport and HM Naval Base at Portsmouth. The
local police inform me that during May 1985
"the Royal Navy were engaged with special radar tests on St Boniface Down,
Ventnor. This involved a Naval Helicopter at times flying from the sea to
St Boniface Down, sometimes at night..."
HM Naval Base directed me to write to you in pursuit of an ordinary explanation
for what is a most unusual sighting. Naturally I don't expect you to give me
the full details of any tests that may have taken place, however, I would
be very grateful if you can answer the following specific questions regarding
military activities in the Ventnor Area:
(a) were exercises carried out near Ventnor on the evening of May I9th?
{b) did any such exercises involve the use of naval helicopters or other
such devices controlled by the personnel involved?
(c) at what times and locations did any exercises occur?
Any other information you can give me, or advice on which authority to
write to, will be gratefully received. Naturally any correspondence will
be treated with due respect and my final report will draw upon any
information you give me. °
I do hope you will find the time to help me with my enquiry, so often

in the past sightings of unusual objects have become a matter of controversy
when in reality an ordinary explanation could have been found with a little
co~operation., I look forward to your reply with interest,

Yours Sincerely,
From: Captain Section 40 Saaz Navy

POI 3LN I3th November 1985

Dear Sir,

I am writing to you on behalf of the British UFO Research
Association, a society that collects, investigates and usually finds
ordinary explanations for reports of unusual aerial phenomena. I have
decided to write to you because of information given to me by Ventnor
Police Station. They tell me that during May 1985, the Royal Navy

‘were engaged with special radar tests on St. Boniface Down,

Ventnor. This involved a Naval helicopter at times flying from

the sea to St. Boniface Down, sometimes at night."
Could you please confirm or deny that

(a) you undertook any such exercises in the Ventnor area during

May 1985, for the purpose stated;
(b) list the times and dates of these tests; and
(c) describe any aspects of the tests that may have resulted in
local residents seeing unusual objects or lights.
The report I am investigating concerns a resident from Lowtherville who
was Walking on the Downs above Rew Close at about 20.40 Hrs on Sunday
I9th May. He observed what he first thought was a parachute about a
kilometer to the south east, the object rose and fell before slowly
travelling up the valley in a north-westerly direction. Because the
object was seen against houses and St Boniface Down I rule out astronomical
objects as the responsible stimulus. The witness describes the UFO as
being disk shaped, with two hazily defined lights, a flange and a slight
glow. He appears to be an ordinary, sane person and has not made financial
gain from his sighting. Obviously a helicopter seen at dusk should be
quite identifiable, even if it was half a mile from the witness. Consequentl;
I am quite prepared to believe that the stimulus for the sighting was a
remote controlled device of some sort, although I realise that this
would not be publically admitted. Nevertheless, it would be very useful
to my investigation if you could respond positively to the questions I
have posed above, and suggest any further line of enquiry. Any help will
be used in my final report and treated with due respect. Thankyou for
your co-operation,
Yours Sincerely,

Ventnor Police Station, I.0.W.
Sunday 27th October 1985

Dear Sir,

I am writing to you on behalf of the British U.F,.0O. Research

Association, a society that collects, analyses and usually explains

UFO reports. Earlier this year we received a report from a resident in

Lowtherville who claims he observed an unusual object in the area directly

above Rew Close, at about 20.40 Hrs on May 19th 1985. The object was disk

shaped, about 8 feet across, and displayed two hazilly defined lights on its

upper surface. The witness appears to be an ordinary, sane and sober person,

and has not attempted to make financial gain from his experience.

Usually, UFO reports turn out to be mundane phenomena that witnesses
observed under unusual conditions, but in this case I am unable to suggest
an ordinary explanation for the sighting. Consequently, I am writing to you
in the hope that you can inform me whether

(a) you received any reports of unusual phenomena in the Ventnor area
during May 1985, particularly on the I9th;
(b) what actions, if any, did you undertake to deal with those reports; and
(c) are you aware of any potential stimuli that may have been responsible
for these reports (eg. helicopters, model aircraft, hang gilders
etc).
Naturally, any correspondence will be treated with due respect. Thank you
for your co-operation,
‘ Yours Sincerely,

{Lam writing to thank you for your recent letter to the Civil Aviation Authority
which has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for a reply.

You mentioned that you are currently in the process of researching the phenomena
of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) and asked if we possessed any material on
this subject which could be released to you to help you with your studies.

I regret that the release of any material on the subject "en bloc't is not possible

as, like other Government bodies, all Ministry of Defence files are subject to

the Public Records Act. As you may be aware, this Act states that official files
generally remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last action

has been taken. I am pleased to say, however, that we are able to answer any specific
queries you may have about particular incidents which have occurred recently and

can let you know whether or not we have any reports on the incident and if so,

what it contains.

Nevertheless, I think it should be noted that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate
some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence
is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence
of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally

the case, we do not attempt to investigate or identify the objects.

As a rule, information that we have on UFOs is limited to the brief details of
sightings passed onto us by those who witnessed the phenomena. These reports are
referred to MOD staff who are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom
and they examine the reports as part of their normal duties. Having said that,
however, since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications,
the MOD does not have any department which is appointed solely for the purpose

of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed on the subject
Full-time. This being the case, we do not have the necessary resources to undertake
any further investigations or general surveys. I am sure you will appreciate that
defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing security of the UK
against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. -

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive, that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them, Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering
the earth's atmosphere, ball lighting, unusual cloud formations, meteorological
balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of other things.
You also asked for any technical sheets on RAF fast jets and I have pleasure in
enclosing a poster showing brief details and specifications of all current RAF

aircraft. For further details and for information on those aircraft of USA and
the Soviet Union, I would suggest you obtain a copy of "Jane's All the Worlds'

Aircraft" from your local reference library.

I hope that this proves useful.

Encl.

January 18th, 1988

Dear Sirs,

Re: Request to inspect non-classified files.

I am seeking permission to inspect reports of alleged
unidentified flying object sightings by members of the public.
I understand that these accounts do not constitute classified
material.

I believe access to the documents concerned is governed by the
Public Records Act, which prohibits public inspection of an
official record for 30 years after the last action has been
taken on it.

My understanding is that the earliest reports of alleged
sightings were forwarded to you in 1957. As no investigation
of such reports has been conducted by the Ministry it would
appear the last action staff carried out on these submissions
occurred shortly after reciept.

If this assumption is wrong, perhaps you could inform me of the
date the reports will be made available. I will then contact
you again at that time. Could you also tell me whether the
Lord Chancellor is empowered to allow an applicant access to
public records even if the application falls within the 30 year
limitation.

I am most grateful for your help in this matter.

Yours faithfully,

Thank you for your letters dated 2¢ and 30 November 1987 which gave details of
UFO sightings seen in Holmfirth and Huddersfield West Yorkshire on 28 October 1987
and 16 November 1987 respectively. You also asked if the MOD possessed any informa-
tion on these sightings, but despite a thorough search through our reccrds, I regret
that I have been unable to find any records of sightings which correspond to the
details which you gave either in Holmfirth, Huddersfield or in the North of England
as a whole.

You may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some
information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to
determine whether or not Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) present a threat to the
security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this
is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects. Information that we have is, therefore, limited to the brief details of
the sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena. These reports
are referred to MOD staff in the departments which are responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part of their normal
duties.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed solely for the
purpose of studying reports of UFOs and we have no staff who are employed .on the
subject full time. Since our interest in UFOs is. limited te possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the.scientific significance of

these phenomena. I am sure you will appreciate that defence funds are already
fully committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily, aggression
from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could not justify the use of

defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security
of the UK had been identified.

°
Nonetheless, it ia clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-

entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, metearo~
logical balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of other

things. i

Thank you for your letter to the Ministry of Defence dated 12 November 1987 which
has been forwarded to me for attention. You asked for information that the MOD
might have on Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) for help with your study for

a GCSE qualification in English.

You might find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate

some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence

is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence

of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not normally

the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.
Information that we have is, therefore, limited to the brief details of the sightings
passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena. These reports are referred

to MOD staff in the departments which are responsible for the air defence of

the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we have
not carried out a study into the scientific significance of these phenomena.

I am sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are already fully committed
to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the
Warsaw Pact countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence
funds on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of

the UK had been indentified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re~entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host

of other things. At present we do not possess any positive evidence that ‘alien
spacecraft' exist or have landed on this planet.

I hope that this is helpful.

Thank you for your letter dated 2 November 1987 to the Prime Minister. This
has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for a reply.

We were interested to read of your experiences and those of your mother and details
of these sightings heve been nected. We have else locked through our records

to see if we have any other reports which correspond to those that you saw.
However, I regret to say that none were found; yours were the first.

Whilst we are interested to receive details of unusual sightings or experiences
such as those witnessed by yourself, I think it might be helpful if I explain
that whilst we do co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not Unidentified
Flying Objects (UFOs) present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do and this is not normally the case, ne
attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects. Information that
we have is, therefore, limited to the brief details of the sightings passed on
to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed solely for

the purpose of studying reports of such objects and we have no staff who are
employed on the subject full time. This being so we do not have the resources

to undertake any further investigation or general survey. The reports we receive
are referred to MOD staff in the department which is responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom and they examine the reports as part of their normal
duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications we do

not carry out full time studies into the scientific significance of these phenomena.
I am sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are fully committed to

the continuing security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw
Pact countries. This being so, we could not justify the use of defence funds

on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of the UK

had been identified.

Nonetheless, it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange things
have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations
could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite debris
re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,

meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host
of other things.

I would also like to thank you for the newspaper cuttings of recent events.

However, we do not have any evidence that points to any of these events being
caused by strange beings or unidentified objects.

I hope that this is of help.

Yours sincerely

Thank you for your recent letter to the Prime Minister enclosing
a copy of a report compiled about a UFO sighting in Devon on the
evening of 4/5 August 1987. This has been passed to MOD and I have
been asked to reply.

Toecelieve that you have also now received a formal reply tron 40

Section 40 all this department in response to a copy of your earlier
letter of 29 August about the same incident. We are grateful for
reeeipt of your report which will remain on our files although I
believe that there is little I can usefully add to
comments on the incident itself. It certainly seems to us that the
most likely explanations for the various reports was that the two
VC10 tankers, which were in North Devon at the time in question on a
refuelling exercise, had been sighted. And in this context it is
perhaps worth adding that local RAF stations will not necessarily be
aware of all air activity in their area as aircraft could have come
from elsewhere in the UK or indeed RAF Germany.

May I also assure you that MOD do not, as you suggest in your
letter to the Prime Minister refuse to release information they hold
on UFOs, and we are happy to respond to any queries about specific
incidents as inRRWMBEN /¢tter of 12 October, We do not however
have sufficient staff to search through our records to provide
comparative co-ordinated data on a range.of incidents in response to
public queries nor could we justify such action given that we do not
generally consider that UFOs present a threat to the security and
defence of this country.

eceive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the

security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that
they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD
to investigate or identify the objects.

appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further
investigation or general surveys. The limited information that we
receive from those who witnessed the phenomena are referred to MOD
staff in the department which ig responsible for the air defence of
the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part of their
normal duties

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of
the UX against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.

believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the

meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles

was, where he came from and other relevant details.

\
se
Thank you for your recent letters to the Prime Minister and the

Ministry of Defence which asked for information on Unidentified
Flying Objects (UFOs). ° I have been asked to reply.

You may find it helpful if I first explain that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or
not UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects. Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief
details of the sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the
phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further
investigations or general surveys. The reports we receive are
referred to MOD staff in the departments which are responsible for
the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports
as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are already fully committed to the continuing
security of the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw
Pact countries. This being so we could not justify the use of
defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to
the security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.
As far as the release of UFO sighting reports is concerned all
MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act. Under this Act
official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30
years after the last action has been taken. Although I regret that
we are unable to release material en bleo, we are able to answer any
specific queries you may have about particular incidents which have
occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a report on
the incident and if so what it contains.

Finally, I would like to assure you that we are not trying in
any way to cbscure the truth about UFOs from the general public and
that our files covering UFO sightings are unclassified.

I ndpe that this proves helpful.
