‘8 December 1987

Thank you for your letter dated 9 October which has been
passed to me for attention. I am sorry for the delay in replying.

You asked for any information that we might possess on a
sighting reported to the local police by a FEW RMMMMEN ©ollowing an
incident in her garden on 4 January 1979. JI regret to say that
despite a thorough search through our records I can find no
correspondence which relates to this particular sighting. I know
that you will find this disappointing but I think it would be helpful
if IT explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some
information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not Unidentified Flying Objects
(UFOs) present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects. Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief
details of the sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the
phenomena. .

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of unidentified
objects, and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full
time. This being so we do not have the resources to undertake any
further investigations or general surveys. The reports we receive
are referred to MOD staff in the Department which is responsible for
the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports
as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of
the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.
Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.

As far as the release of our reports en bloc is concerned all
MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act. Under this Act
official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30
years after the last action has been taken. However, we are able to
answer any specific queries you may have about particular incidents
which haye occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a
report on the incident and if so what it contains.

I hope that this is useful. ; ;

Thank you for your letter dated 9 November, which asked for
information on the identity of MOD distribution points.

i think it would be helpful if IT explain that the Air Staff
Secretariat is the MOD point of contact for those members of the
general public whe wish to enquire about the subject of Unidentified
Flying Objects (UFOs). Any enquiries received elsewhere in MOD will
be referred to us for action, and will clearly be subject to delays
in transfer. 1 would be grateful therefore if you would address any
subsequent queries you may have to this branch only.

We are happy to help although we are unable to release material
"en bloc" as all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act.
Under this Act official files generally remain closed from publics
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. However,
we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have oceurred recently and can let you
know whether we have a report on the incident and if so what it
contains.

To hope that this proves useful.

i

Thank you for your recent letters which asked for information
that the Ministry of Defence might have on Unidentified Flying
Objects (UFOs) and on a variety of ether subjects.

You may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive
and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not
UFOs present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the
case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the
objects. Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief
details of the sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the
phenomena.

We also have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
peing so we do not have the resources to undertake searches through
our archive material to answer general queries or offer this sort of
answering service to the public. However, as I mentioned in my
letter of 15 October, if you’ have any specific queries about a
particular incident which has occurred recently we can’ let you know
if we have a report for it and if so what it contains.

Although I regret that I am unable to answer the majority of
your questions on UFOs because of the lack of detail I have looked
through our files to see if we received any reports for the incidents
you say occurred on 12 March and 9 October 1986. Unfortunately this
was to no avail.

Il regret that I am also unable to answer any of your questions
on the other subjects that you asked about as we do not have the
facilities and information available here in the Air Staff
Secretariat to provide you or other members of the public with an
answer. I would therefore suggest that if you wish to pursue your
enquiries further, you should contact the Director of Public
Relations office here in the Ministry of Defence; the Public Records
Office at Kew, London; your local Library or a nearby Career Advisory
Service office.

loam sorry that I am unable to help further.

4 December 1987

Thank you for your letter dated 19 October to RAF Rudloe Manor
which requested information on a possible Unidentified Flying Object
(UFO) sighting seen in the Harrogate area on 14 July 1987. I nave
been asked to reply on their behalf as the Air Staff Secretariat is
the MOD point of contact for the general public on this particular
subject.

To have looked through our files and can confirm that we received
two reports which correspond to the details given in your letter, one
of which was the report originally sent to RAF Rudloe Manor.

Although we are able to answer any specific queries you may have
about particular incidents which have occurred recently and let you
know whether we have a report on the incident, I regret that the
release of this material en bloc is not possible. This is because
ali MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act which means that
official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30
years after the last action has been taken.

IT hope that this proves useful.

Dear Sir or Madan,

i wonder if you occuld help me? I would be interested in hearing if your
department had any ‘UPO' sightings reported to you for the evening of the 28th
of Octaber 1987, I hava been informed that an unidentified aerial object was
seen ‘hovering’ in the sky about 1-1% miles out of Holmfirth (Weet Yorkshire) on
the A695 Greenfield road at about 800 feat above the road. The phenomena was
displaying a ring of red, orange and white lights around it and stayed in place
for at least ten minutes before moving off slowly over the moors to the south.

I would be very grateful if you could inform me if you had this sighting

D/Sec(AS)12/3

Date

fy) November 1987

Thank you for your letter dated 10 October to RAF Fylingdales
which has been passed to me for attention in my capacity as the MOD
point of contact on the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects
(UFOs). You asked for any information that we might possess on two
sightings seen in Yorkshire on 28 and 29 September 1987.

You may find it helpful if I first explain that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the
main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or
not Unidentified Flying Objects present a threat to the security and
defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and
this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investi-
gate or identify the objects. Information that we have ig therefore
limited to the brief details of the sightings passed on to us by
those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
being so we do not have the. resources to undertake any further
investigation or general survey. The reports we receive are referred
to MOD staff in the Department which is responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part
of their normal duties. IT regret to say that we did not receive any
reports which relate to the details you gave in your letter and T can
confirm that the RAF did not see anything unusual on the nights in
question.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of
the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.
Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.

T hope that this proves useful.

Dear Aut 4

I have been engaged for some months past on a series of articles for the
Sunday Express on Unidentified Fiying Objects. The method has been to choose
Yhree or four individual experiences where someone of integrity and credibility
has had a sighting so far unexplained.

My researches have brought me into contact with many investigators of this
kind of phenomenon, and all seem to have failed to get a satisfactory answer ~
and sometimes no answer at all ~ to their enquiries of the Ministry of Defence.

The incidents I have researched are:

1. Police-Constable of Todmorden, on the Lancs/Yorks border,
in the early hours of Friday February 28th 1980.

2, Two schoolgirls, EERMCMROM ond EERIEGZMIMEE in tho Meanvood Valley,

Leeds, on Thursday November 22nd 1979. Here an purported + n 40
the Minietry of Defence apparently visited the y four days later, on
or about Monday the 26th, and interviewed the two girls and the father of one of
them. Can you confirm this? I have only a verbal, and perhaps ineccurate,
version of what took place.
3. A resident of Upper Ventnor, on the Isle of wight, witnessed the strange
flight of a disc~like and illuminated object in the Upper Ventnor valley just
before duak on th°1985, from a vantage point above the valley. A 0%
investigator, Becton 40] has completed a report on this incident, in which

the following is quoted from a letter from the Ventnor Police:

"Nothing unusual was reported during May 1985. However during this time
the Royal Navy were engaged with special radar tests on St Boniface Down, Ventnor.
This involved a Navalhelicopter at times flying from the sea to St Boniface Down,
sometimes at night.’

On enquiry trom BERMOM OE Portsnouth Naval Base referred him to the Ministry
of Defence, to vhom am enquiry was addressed on November 27th 1985. I em told
that no reply was ever received.

Present plans are that the series of articles will start on Sunday week,
November 22nd, and run for three weeks. dre you able to comment. on any of the
threa specific incidents, and on the UFO phenomenon in general, and if so, could
I please have such comments by early next week if convenient, for possible

inclusion in the articles if agreed?

I look forward to hearing your reaction.

Features Editor,
Sunday Express

doe ON

Thank you for your recent letter which was addressed to Mr
Ross, who has now moved on to take up a new appointment. I have
taken his place.

You asked for any information that we might have as a sighting
seen in the Burnley and Pendle areas on Thursday 10 September 1987.
Having checked through our records I regret to say that we did not
receive any reports which corresponded to the details that you gave.
I have also established that there was no air defence activity,
manoeuvres or exercises in these areas on the night in question.
Indeed, the RAF and NATO do not conduct exercises over major urban
areas at any time.

On a more general note you may find it helpful if I explain
that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO
sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to
determine whether or not Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) present a
threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we
judge that they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is
made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects. Information that
we have is therefore limited to the brief details of the sightings
passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further
investigation or general survey. The reports we receive are referred
to MOD staff in the Department which is responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part
of their normal duties. ;

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of
the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.
Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.

As far as the release of this material en bloc is concerned
all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act. Under this Act
official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30
years after the last action has been taken. However, we are able to
answer any specific queries you may have about particular incidents
which have occurred recently and can let you know whether we have a
report on the incident and if so what it contains.

I hope that this is of help.

Thank you for your letter dated 28 September which has been
passed to me for attention.

You asked for any information that we might have on sightings
made in the Derby and Uttoxeter areas on Sunday 16 and Monday 17
August 1987. Having checked through our records I regret to say that
we did not receive any reports which correspond to the details that
you gave. I also found out that there was no air defence activity,
Maneouvres or exercising in these areas on the two nights in
question. Indeed, the RAF and NATO do not conduct exercises over
major urban areas at any time.

You also asked for copies of reports sent in during August
1987. Although I am able to confirm that we did receive some reports
in August I am sorry to say that the release of this material en bloc
is not possible as all MOD files are subject to the Public Records
Act. Under this Act official files generally remain closed from
public viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken.
However, we are able to answer any specific questions you may have
about particular incidents which have occurred recently (like your
query above) and can let you know whether we have a report on the
incident and if so, what it contains.

On a more géneral note you may find it helpful if I explain
that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO
sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence is to
determine whether or not Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) present a
threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we
judge that they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is
made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects. Information that
we have is therefore limited to the brief details of the sightings
passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is
appointed solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects,
and we have no staff who are employed on the subject full time. This
being so we do not have the resources to undertake any further
investigation or general survey. The reports we receive are referred
to MOD staff in the Department which is responsible for the air
defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part
of their normal duties.
Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
significance of these phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate
that defence funds are fully committed to the continuing security of
the UK against, primarily, aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries.
This being so we could not justify the use of defence funds on _
scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the security of
the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that
many strange things have been observed in the sky, although we
believe that rational explanations could probably be found for most
of them. Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the
earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations,
meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles
or a host of other things.

I note that one of your witnessesses managed to take a

photograph of the object. I'm sure that it will prove interesting
and I would therefore like to take up your kind offer of the loan of

a copy.
I hope that this reply proves useful.

IT return your stamped addressed envelope.

Dear , : ; :
Please find enclosed a copy of a Ufo report that we have just
completed.

As you will see this was a significant sighting of a momentous Ufo.
Ufos exist.We are net trying to convince anyone of this,the proof of their ex-
istence is there in abundance for anyone who seriously wishes to investigate
into the subject.

As a group,we simply aim to record Ufo activity in this area;to hel;
witnesses who may have difficulty in coming to terms with a sighting; and to
publicise any significant Ufo events from this area,Hence this report.

A point I raise a couple of times in the report,and one I feel of
erucial importance to the field of Ufo investigation and its implications, is
this-how do the authorities KNOW that Ufos are not a threat to us?

: I believe this to be true,but why are the authorities and successive
governments convinced that this is so,

If they know there is no threat from Ufos,HOW do they know?And more
interestingly,if they know that,what else do they know?!!

And why are we,the public,the employers of the authorities denied
this informaticn about. these mysterious visitors,It has already been said by
the authorities that Ufos present no threat to national security,so on what
grounds do these public servants refuse us the information they hold,for us?

Hope this report is of some interest,or can be of some use to you,
If not would it be possible for you to reurn it to me,f8o that I can forward
to someone else,

We would be interested in any comments or information that you would.
care to make on this sighting,or on Ufes in general.I would be interested in
amy knowing what the political party line(s) are on the subject.

Best wishes,

11.20 p.m. se

to forma triingle shape
composed of many lights —

mostly white but with some ~

red flashing ones,” he said.

I saw a man looking at the object, but when he turned and saw me he hurried off, At the
end of the road I crossed Pemross Road and into the car park overlooking the Tamar River.
The lights were now at the other end of the bridge and just above it. I called to two
men who were sitting in a car, and they came over. I pointed out the UFO to them and
they were both very excited about it, There was a telescope in the car park and I put
some money it (thinking how appropriate!!), but unfortunately nothing happened so I put
in another coin but still no good, The UFO was now very, very slowly flying,over the
top of the bridge and heading slightly to our right towards the Armaments Depot that is
next to the bridge. One of the men said that it was heading for the Arms Depot, I took
2 photos as it flew over the bridge. It took 3-4 mins to cross the river, .

I now lost sight of it as it flew into the glare of the lights of the toll booths on

As I walked towards it the light again divided into 2 smaller lights and started to

than the right and vice versa, constantly changing position, but, until what happened
next, I thought that they were only one object.

i slowly walked ahead, camera at the ready, but although the object was moving, it was

"at 10. 10pm.....my eye was caught by the lights of a Tri-Star jet airl-
iner coming over our heads and heading for Exeter airport.Usually if jets are
heading in to land at Ex. they are down to & or 10,000ft by the time they go

this huge double cross,illuminated like a fairground,coming over the roof tops
towards us.

"The jet airliner passed underneath this huge shape.I should imagine it
was no more than 2000ft. above the plane.In comparison of size,i est.that althc
ugh the Ufo was at least 2000ft. above the jet,it would have been as wide as
4 jets wingtip to wingtip,and each cross(+)arm was equal..in length and breadtk
So as we saw it(the 2 crosses joined together}it was about 8 times the wingsps
of the jet in length,and 4 times in breadth, (Illus. 12. ) :

"It was at about 10,000ft,travelling at no more than 100-150mph. ,over us
from NE to SW(heading for Flymouth.BB)taking a good 15mins from horizon to
horizon.

"At 10.30pm I phoned Exet.Airport,to find out if the pilot of the plane

ing.I pointed out that the military had said that there wasnt any of their air
craft flying at that time and he said that he would put me through to the man
who was on duty that evening,which he did. :

This man with a rather sarcastic attitiude said that they werent Ufos.I
had asked him if he could give me info on height and size of opjects ard he
said that these were milit.planes refuelling, Again I said that the milit. had
denied this and he replied that he knew what they were because he had seen the:
I asked if he had seen.them on radar or with the naked eye and at this point
another man cut into the convers&tion and said that HE had actually been on
duty that night and could state catergorically that the objects seen were mili.
pianes refuelling and that every month RAF Brise Norton sent flight times of
refuelling aircraft to all civil airports so that they would know when to exp-
ect them.Fine,I said could you just give me the details of the time ,height and
speed etc,for the craft that were seen that night.He replied that he didnt havi
any details for that night but could give me details for the following Friday.
I said-I could hardly see the point of that but if he could give me details foi
the 4th I would be grateful and could see how they tied in with the reports I
had.He repeated that he had no info for that evening.I asked why that was if
Brise Norton furnished info for all refuelling flights at which point another
man cut in and said that HE had actually been on duty that night and could stai
state that the objs.were planes without any doubt.I realised that I was not
going to get anywhere with this so thanked them for their help and rung off.

Following this I wrote to the station commanders at RAF Mt.Wise,Plymouth,
RAF St. Mawgan,Newquay,C'wall;RNAS Culdrose,Helston,C'wall.See enclosed letter,

and said he couldnt help because their radars did not cover the Plym/Ext. area
cut that he was sending my most interesting letter to the MOD in London and
shat he was sure that they would contact me "in due course"(To date. 29/9.1 have
‘heard from them).The other commander who replied that"I can confirm that the
2 delta shaped objs.sightied were a pair of RAF VC 10 aircraft carrying out

of 1, t,they are indeed very similar to the Jumbo jet(195ft) ;

be reassured and communicate this explanation to your associates".

Section 4 hai day after the event.I have omitted all names to prevent any pees
ible embarassment or repercussions on the military/civil staff involved.

The truly staggering sighting by and his friends must be
one of the most impressive on record.As he states"My drawing is exactly what
it looked like"He was watching the Ufos through a 45x teles. then drawing it,

pass over,and as can be seen he is quite a gifted artist.

size of the individual Ufos.How the obvious tremendous size of the Ufos ties

work out.Maybe if,when I was in the tele.box I had looked up into the sky,inst-
ead of concentrating on the lights directly in front of me,I might have seen
the colossal darkened bulk of these unimaginable Ufos.

that had came from the larger ones,but when a little while later I saw the lig-
lights, low over Saltash,they were the same as the ones that had passed the tel.
box and remained the same,except glowing more and more brightly,until nearly
overhead when they showed themaelves as the"Christmas tree",

unable to clarify,and becasue of the size of the report will leave that to
others.At the moment my prime concern is to get the report out within a reas-
onable time after the event.

Could I also mention a point that has intrigued me for some years,It is
illustrated by the statement to the Exmouth Herald by an RAF spokesman that
unless Ufos are hovering over an RAF base,"they are-of no interest to us"!!!

I have been led to:- believe by the Govt.for at least 8yrs. that we are in
constant danger of an attack by Russia and that it is essential that we main-
tain our military defences,especially the RAF,to a high level of alertness and
battle readiness,

Yet here we have a case of "foreign" air-craft,repeatedly flying over
one of our top nuclear defence installations(and we have many other reports of
the same,particularly over Bull Point Armaments Depot)and the people we are
paying very well indeed,and investing huge amounts of money,to keep our air
Space secure,when confronted with"alien"aircraft of possibly hostile intent,
are HOT INTERESTED! !!This amazes me, ‘ . .

Will some one,preferably someone with some access to the truth,explain
to me the point of a defence system,that is costing billions of pounds every ,.
year,yet when it is presented with"aliens"in our air-—space,are“not interested,

Unless the RAF investigate air intrusionshow are they so sure that we
are not in imminent danger of attack?How will they tell the difference between:
Jfos playfully gambolling around our skies,and enemy aircraft intent on dest-
cuction?HOW DO THEY KNOW THE DIFFERENCE??

of a rank that I felt may be prepared or able to answer the question,and as
zan be seen in the letter to the Air Bases brought up the topic with several
questions, All questions of this nature that I have asked in the past and in
the letter have been studiously ignored, .

2pt Ufos as a non-threat?0r on the other side of the coin-what is the point of.

ided by unknown,possibly hostile air-craft.This is a very significant question
ind one which sooner or later the Govt.must address,because otherwise what is
the point of the RAF???

Any comments would be of interest-particularly Official comments.

This is not in any way an attack or criticism on the RAF,for whom we

have the greatest respect.

Thank you for your letter of 25 August to the Chief of the Air
Staff. Ihave been asked to reply on his behalf.

Iwas interested to read of your experience whilst serving
with the RAP in the Far East during 1952, and. have copied your letter
to a number of branches within the Ministry of Defence to see if they
have any other reports of this incident. I am afraid however, that
none had any such records or indeed any papers relating to the
sighting you described. We do, as you may already know have papers
on the HRendlesham Forest incident which you mentioned and would be
happy to provide you with these, shonid you wish.

foam afraid however that I have to confirm that the Ministry
of Defence's interest in UFOs relates only to whether or not they
present a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom.
Unless we judge that they do pose such a threat, and this is
primarily an air defence matter, no further action ig taken, Ifa
sighting is considered worth investigating, and this is not normally
the case, steps may be taken to contact local units and see whether
anything unusual appeared on radar screens. But, the Ministry of
Defence has no other interest in investigating the cause or source of
the phenomenon.

foam sorry that I cannot be more helpful.

Thank you for your recent letter which I read with interest.

You asked for any information that the Ministry of Defence might have
on Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs), especially any reports which relate
to sightings made in the Birmingham area during 1978. As I mentioned in my
jast letter, I regret that the release of materials "en bloc" is not
possible as all MOD files are subject to the Public Reeords Act. Under this
Act official files generally remain closed from publie viewing for 30 years
after the last action has been taken. Also we cannot answer general queries
which would require a great deal of searching and coordination as we simply
do not have the resources to undertake this sort of service for the public.
However, if you have any specific queries about a particular incident which
has occurred recently we can let you know if we have a report for it, and if
so, what it contains. You also asked several, more varied questions and I
will answer these in turn.

For full details of military aircraft performance and their roles, TI
would suggest that you obtain a copy of a pictorial reference book such as
“Jane's: All The World's Aircraft". A copy of this or other similar books
should be available in most bookshops or in your local Library. Moving on
to your next question, we have no objections to the taking of photographs of
military alreraft when they are made available for this purpose, such as at
air displays and air fairs, ete. MACH one is the term for the speed of
sound, which is 762 miles per hour at sea level. Many of our fast jets can
fly at MACH one or faster and therefore are canable of breaking the sound
barrier.

The Official Secrets Act is a large government document which is
published by Her Majesty's Stationery Office (HMSO). It is still available
from them at The HMSO Publications Centre, 51 Nine Elms Lane, Vauxhall,
London SW8 5DR or from a few selected bookshops, I should warn you however
that it is quite expensive!
The helicopter in the 'AIRWOLF' TV series is not a production aircraft
used in 'real life' and as such does not exist outside of that TV programme.

You also asked for a list of service ranks and a copy of the report
written by Lt Col Charles Halt USAF describing the "Rendlesham Forest"
sighting of December 1982. I attach copies of both for your information.

Your trial question asked whether or not it was legal to have a copy of
Pater Wright's book "Spycatcher". Although it is not officially available
in the United Kingdom, it is quite legal for you to possess a copy should
you be able to obtain one.

Finally, I am sending you a poster of RAF aircraft currently in service
which will give you details of their use, performance and roles. I hope
that this is of help.

information on the "Churchill Documents" which relate to the subject
of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs). Although we have been unable
to trace these papers within the Ministry of Defence, we have passed

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE, pea Je cain _

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS}/12/3
Date

Lg October 1987

Thank you for your letter dated 1 October which has been forwarded to
me for attention.

You asked for any information that the United Kingdom Ministry of
Defence might possess on the subject of Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs).
It might help if I first explain that whilst we do receive and co-ordinate
some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not UFOs, as reported to us, present a
threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge
that they do, and this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by the
Department to investigate or identify the objects which have been seen.
Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief details of the
the sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed
solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects, and we have no
staff who are employed on the subject full time. This being so we do not
have the resources to undertake any further investigations or general
surveys. The reports we receive are referred to MOD staff in the
Departments which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom,
and they examine the reports as part of their normal duties.

Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
we have not carried out a study into the scientific significance of these
phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are already
fully committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily,
aggression from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could not
justify the use of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a
clear threat to the security of the UK had been identified.
Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that
rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere,
ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft
lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of other things.

As far as the release of there reports "en bloc" is concerned all MOD
files are subject to the Public Records Act. Under this Act official files
generally remain closed from public viewing for 30 years after the last
action has been taken. However, we are able to answer any specific queries

you may have about particular incidents which have occurred recently and ca
let you know whether we have reports on the incident and if so what it

contains.

To assist you further, please find attached the addresses of five UK

Thank you for your letter dated 29 August to Royal Air Force St Mawgan.

Tt has now been passed to me and I have been asked to reply on behalf of the
Ministry of Defence.

In response to your description of a sighting seen in Devon on the
evening of the 4/5 August 1987 I have checked through our records and can
confirm that we have two reports which correspond to the details you gave.
I attach copies of these reports for your information.

Before I try and answer any of your more specific questions, I think
you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive and co-
ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main concern of the
Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not Unidentified Flying
Objects (UFOs) present a threat to the security and defence of the United
Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally the case,
no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.
Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief details of the
sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

As with all other sighting reports, the report relating to this
particular sighting and your letter havebeen referred to the staff in the
department who are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and
these have been examined as part of their normal duties, Those reports that
are considered to warrant further investigation from an air defence view-
point, and that of course is our only interest, prompts us to enquire of
units in the locality of the sighting whether anything unusual was seen on
radar or if they know of any flying activities. In this particular case
nothing unusual was seen and no aircraft were sent up to investigate the
phenomena. I am sure you will therefore understand that we do not have any
details regarding the size, height or speed of the object. However, I can
confirm that two VC10 tanker aircraft were involved in a refuelling exercise
during the evening of 4/5 August over North Devon and it is therefore quite
possible that the objects you mention were these aircraft.
The departments within the MOD which have an interest in air defence
activities have considered these sightings and they are satisfied that they
did not present a threat to the defence and security of the United Kingdom.
Nonétheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many strange
things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that rational
explanations could probably be found for most of them. Possibilities inelude
satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual
cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at
unusual angles or a host of other things.

T hope that this is of some help.

roe Goconel 4

Thank you for your letter dated 4 September which was addressed to
Royal Air Force Chivenor and has been passed to the Ministry of Defence for
reply. .

I was interested to read about your accountof an unidentified object
which you say was seen in Devon on 3 August 1987. I have looked through our
records and can confirm that we received two reports from the West Country
for 3/4 August which correspond to the details you gave. I attach copies of
these reports for your information, although in keeping with our policy, I
have deleted the identity of the informants. I would also like to confirm
that two VC10 tanker aircraft were involved in a refuelling exercise in
North Devon during the evening of the 4 August. It is therefore quite
possible that the objects you mention were these RAF aircraft.

On a more general note you may find it helpful if I explain that whilst
we do receive and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not Unidenti-
fied Flying Objects (UFOS) present a threat to the security and defence of
the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and this is not normally
the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investigate or identify the objects.
Information that we have is therefore limited to the brief details of the
sightings passed on to us by those who witnessed the phenomena.

We have no department in the Ministry of Defence which is appointed
solely for the purpose of studying reports of such objects, and we have no
staff who are employed on the subject full time. This being so we do not
have the resources to undertake any further investigations or general
surveys. The reports we receive are referred to MOD staff in the Deprtments
which are responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom, and they
examine the reports as part of their normal duties.
Since our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence implications
we have no carried out a study into the scientifie significance of these
phenomena. I'm sure that you will appreciate that defence funds are fully
committed to the continuing security of the UK against, primarily, aggression
from the Warsaw Pact countries. This being so we could not justify the use
of defence funds on scientific investigations, unless a clear threat to the
security of the UK had been identified.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports that we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe that
rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere,
ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft
lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of other things.

I hope that this is of some help.

Yone Gremiah,

Thank you for your recent letter which described a sighting a
lorry driver made whilst travelling between Skipton and Harrogate on
23 December 1983.

As I mentioned in my letter of 20 August, we only investigate
reported sightings to see if they present a threat to the defence and
security of the United Kingdom. In this particular case I have found
out that we did not receive a sighting report and as we were unaware
of the incident no attempt was made to investigate it from here.
Nonetheless I can assure you that the department within the Ministry
of Defence which is responsible for the air defence of the United
Kingdom constantly monitors UK airspace to ensure the continuing
defence and security of the United Kingdom. ;

I hope that this is of some help.

Thank you for your letter dated 15 September 1987 which
requested information on possible UFO sightings reported in the South
East region during the period 1980 to 1987.

As I mentioned in my letter dated 5 August 1987 we are able to
answer specific queries you may have about particular incidents which
have occurred recently and let you know whether we have any reports,
and if so what they contain. However, we can not answer more general
queries which would require a great deal of searching and co-
ordination as we simply do not have the resources to undertake this
sort of service for the public.

i am sorry I cannot be more helpful.

Dear

Thank you for the report of an unusual sighting you and your
family witnessed from your back garden on 4 September 1987 which has
been passed to me by Chesterfield Police Station.

You may find it helpful if I explain that whilst we do receive
and co-ordinate some information about UFO sightings, the main
concern of the Ministry of Defence is to determine whether or not
Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs) present a threat to the security
and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and
this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investi-
gate or identify the objects.

I have referred your report to the staff in the department
which is responsible for the air defence of the United Kingdom and it
has been examined as part of their normal duties. The Department is
satisfied that the events you described are of no defence
significance.

Nonetheless it is clear from the reports.we receive that many
strange things have been observed in the sky, although we believe
that rational explanations could probably be found for most of them.
Possibilities include satellite debris re-entering the earth's
atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual cloud formations, meteorological
balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft at unusual angles or a host of
other things.

T hope that this is of value.

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3

Date |
29 September 1987

Thank you for your letter dated 3 September which enclosed
your notebook recording the details of various strange happenings
which you have encountered over the last few years. It made
fascinating reading.

As you are already aware, the main concern of the Ministry of
Defence is to determine whether or not unidentified sightings present
a threat to the security and defence of the United Kingdom. This
being the case our knowledge of UFO and alien phenomena is extremely
limited and therefore I can't possibly begin to offer you an
explanation for your experiences.

Like yourself, the Ministry of Defence has an open mind on the
UFO subject but to date we have received no indisputable evidence
that aliens have landed on this planet. Nonetheless, we are grateful
to you for writing about your experiences and you might be interested

to know that we received no other reports which correspond to the
details that you gave.

I hope you will find this helpful.
AS Kacey

Your reference

Our reference
D/Sec(AS)12/3
Date

29September 1987

Thank you for your letter which provided details of a sighting
you and your friends made on 13 April 1987. I have looked through
our records and I regret that I do not have any further information
on this particular sighting; yours is the first report.

You may find it helpful if I first explain that whilst we do
receive and co-ordinate some information about Unidentified Flying
Ovject (UFO) sightings, the main concern of the Ministry of Defence
is to determine whether or not UFOs present a threat to the security
and defence of the United Kingdom. Unless we judge that they do, and
this is not normally the case, no attempt is made by MOD to investi-
gate or identify the objects. Information that we have is therefore
limited to the brief details of the sightings passed on to us by
those, such as yourself, who witnessed them. The reports are then
referred to MOD staff in the Department which is responsible for the
air defence of the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as
part of their normal duties. Your report will be sent shortly.

Although our interest in UFCs is limited it is clear from the
reports that we receive that many strange things have been observed
in the sky, although we believe that rational explanations could
probably be found for most of them. Possibilities include satellite
debris re-entering the earth's atmosphere, ball lightning, unusual
cloud formations, meteorological balloons, aircraft lights, aircraft
at unusual angles or a host of other things.

T can confirm that we received about 120 reports during the
last year and since September 1986 we have had 17 from the Yorkshire,
area. However I regret that the release of this material en bloc is
not possible as all MOD files are subject to the Public Records Act.
Under this Act official files generally remain closed from public
viewing for 30 years after the last action has been taken. However,
we are able to answer any specific queries you may have about
particular incidents which have occurred recently and can let you
know whether we have a report on the incident and if so what it
contains.

Finally I would like to assure you that there is no question
of attempting to cover up any incident, nor are we trying in any way
to obscure the truth about UFOs from the general public.

I hope you will find this helpful.

Baas Gnu Ctretiby
